  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
## 13. SEC4: independent intent and usable default campaigns (2026-10-19)

Implementer: Codex, SEC4, 2026-10-19. Wave 18 found 192/231 row
invariants copied enforcement, no audit use, and one campaign run. DX4 owns
staging discovery/provisioning and QRY owns query return/hidden lookup semantics.

### 13.1 Intent and diagnostics

An advisory N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE identifies table/column effect intent
whose bound predicate and effective gate imply each other in the existing
three-valued kernel. Equality is advice only: neither the note nor a generated
candidate establishes independent specification intent. Keep existing valid
assertions and proof statuses; do not reject a legitimate simple owner policy.
Suggest reviewing the intended role, resource relationship, sensitive column
and lifecycle condition. Draft candidates are explicitly unconfirmed questions
with per-role/per-column policy alternatives, never copies of the effective
gate. Where no independent candidate is available, emit a question rather than
inventing intent. Starters explain the product requirement and show a distinct
policy decomposition (for example member/admin alternatives).

### 13.2 Generated initial worlds and password-only test

Without explicit fixture files, generate isolated deterministic typed fixtures:
two distinct principals per discovered role and service seat, related resource,
membership and ownership rows, independent principal fields, enum alternatives,
and verified image/file uploads. Ordinary seed validation, constraints, defaults,
maintenance and reference ordering remain mandatory. Declined populations name
their coverage gap; continue with the validated empty world if no population
can be admitted. Existing explicit fixtures retain their meaning.

GeneratedFixtureTrace is distinct from AuthoredFixtureTrace: it establishes an
actual authored operation/read from a validated generated initial fixture,
**not** an application operation sequence that creates that initial fixture.
A replayed policy violation from either fixture origin fails acceptance. Seed-
only and synthetic table probes remain supplemental/non-failing. Every trace
retains the full initial rows and actual arguments; reports label this origin.
The same test-only in-memory engine path works with password-only schemas;
never open development doors or change deployed identity configuration. HTTP
authentication stays separately exercised through the existing isolated serve.
A fixture-free default test may pass its generated campaigns without pretending
that application business cases ran. Saved-source and explicit business-case
requirements retain their existing contract.

### 13.3 Coverage and bounded checking

Column update intent is spelled `invariant table.column update only P`; existing
`write` still includes insert and update. Multi-write observations may judge a
unique explicit target write when other stable writes touch different tables;
ambiguous repeated target writes and unstable policy dependencies stay unproved.
Histories interleave actors, argument alternatives, relationship changes and
successor operations. Media disclosure uses compiler-typed physical payload
witnesses; alternate-world media comparisons need a coupled verified ledger.
Role-wide branches gated by row values receive advisory N_ROLE_WIDE_READ when
the table carries private intent or media; suggest a projection/board table.
Escalation advice must constrain the assigned role by dominating argument guards
before naming it; an excluded target is not a witness.

`check` performs the sound static analysis and a bounded concrete campaign;
work exhaustion is explicit unproved coverage and points to `test`. A shared
short campaign deadline bounds exploration, including observer/world loops.
Already replayed violations remain failures; timeout never awards proof.
Memoizing exact implication input pairs is allowed; the cache is bounded,
process-local, and stores only the existing kernel's result. No assumptions
about fixture rows enter static proofs. `test` and `audit` retain the full
campaign bounds. Target cold/warm median check latency is at most five seconds
on the fifty wave-18 schemas.

Default check output is one short summary block with invariant counts and at
most five ranked actionable notes with source lines and concrete next steps.
Full reasons, individual unproved paths and complete note lists require
`--verbose` (JSON retains complete structured evidence). Refuted reachable
witnesses remain visible in compact form. Recommend `snapback4 test` when
exploration remains and `snapback4 audit` when exposures need review. The
start packet/cheatsheet teach check → test → audit as the ordinary loop.

### 13.4 Qualification

Pin before/after measurements, all fifty fixture-free campaign outcomes, the
eleven high-lane spec-intent outcomes, and the 200-schema no-false-refusal
survey. Preserve frozen lane sources. Run changed snippets/starters through
packed CLI qualification, the full requested crate/client/differential suites,
Linux qualification, affected Mac tests, wasm/ref/docs/root checks. Reports
distinguish actual caught, advisory/partial, and uncovered findings. No release
version bump, push or publication belongs to SEC4.

### 13.5 Review clarifications (revision 2)

This amendment supersedes §12.2's authored-fixture-only failure rule: both
fixture origins fail on replayed authored observations, with explicit origin in
every compact failure line, JSON, counterexample selection and count. Generated
initialization remains a trusted test assumption, not proof of empty-state
reachability. Synthetic table operations and trusted-seed-only assertions still
do not fail. `apply` prefers an authored fixture trace, then a generated fixture
trace, then supplemental witnesses; retain the shortest trace within each origin.
Qualification must analyze each new generated-origin refusal, including schemas
with no authored invariants. No generated coverage yields Proved or a zero-hole
claim. The 200-schema survey defines a false refusal as a new refusal without a
replayed violation of independently confirmed intent (invariant-free schemas
must have zero new refusals). Pin each high lane's source hash and caught/partial/
not-caught finding table, and each fixture-free campaign's result and origin.

A default fixture-free test with no saved-source selection clears `no_cases`
only after its generated campaigns execute; it always prints `not covered: no
authored business cases; generated-fixture only`. Explicit fixtures and saved
sources retain the business-case rule; `--fixture-only` keeps its meaning.

Check has a shared deterministic 512 call/world reservation budget, in addition
to a 1500 ms safety deadline. Each generated world, history replay step and
observer call reserves a unit (paired observers reserve their four calls).
Existing full-campaign caps remain upper bounds. Ordering is deterministic,
with initial authored typed read observers and column pairs before history
prefixes, then remaining synthetic worlds. The test-only operation matrix remains excluded
from check; `test` and `audit` use full campaign bounds. Exhaustion names `check
concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run
snapback4 test for the full campaign` (or `time budget exhausted`). Counts and
complete uncovered details remain in JSON/verbose. No operation result or policy
judgment cache is added: only exact full RuleExpr implication input pairs are
memoized process-locally, bounded to 4096 entries.

The compact block always counts proved/refuted/unproved, labels supplemental
seed/synthetic evidence non-failing, and lists **all** failing invariant sites
with fixture origin and one operation. Notes sort by existing note_rank tier, then security consequence (escalation,
role-wide disclosure, other correctness, mirror, broad-read, missing intent),
then site/code/message, show at most five, and print an explicit remainder count
with `--verbose`/JSON. It must never say pass/ok beside a failing exit.

The mirror comparison uses the row read AND applicable private-owner gate for
column reads, the insert/delete rule for those effects and the judged update
gate for updates. Both directions require proved implication on Readable;
unknown/cap exhaustion emits no equivalence note. This replaces §10.3's
rule-copy draft guidance. Current enforcement remains available in audit; the
draft contains only independent unconfirmed alternatives and spec questions.
Column update grammar is `invariant table.column update only P`: update arm
only, both old and next images, with no whole-table syntactic coverage. Column
write still covers insert plus update. Multi-write reductions retain the full
§12.7/12.8 transitive implicit-target and policy stability closure.

### 13.6 Implementation qualification clarifications

Fixture-free execution retains both empty and generated initial states. The
empty state gets 64 single/pair history slots and the populated state gets 448,
sharing the existing total 512 singles/512 pairs plus observer reservations.
Empty-start traces retain AuthoredFixtureTrace because no synthetic initial
rows are assumed. Related grant/membership/ownership tables include every
sampled actor; payload tables need independent owner/resource rows and enum/
optional alternatives. Optional time/relationship values include null. Text
arguments include typed authored comparison literals; integer arguments include
bounds/changed values. Candidate ordering interleaves domains before truncation.
No new runtime allowance, query result cache or policy judgment cache is added.

Column paired observers prune only programs outside the transitive maintained,
through-parent and raw-rule observer closure; every retained experiment still
validates both worlds, fixed arguments, policy falsehood and repeatability.
Image/file alternatives upload into one fixture ledger fork shared by both
worlds. Once/immutable media uses a fresh validated store with that ledger.
Ambiguous/unsupported paths remain named, never proved. Initial disclosure
witnesses cannot be replaced by unrelated later prefixes.

The corpus survey reports original source exits separately from intent-quality
corrections on disposable copies: an omitted authorized service seat and a
private whole-row declaration over intentionally public occupancy totals are
declared-policy contradictions, not reasons to silently exempt seats or ignore
aggregate observations. The latter requires column privacy intent; the former
requires the intended seat explicitly. Preserve original hashes, exact witnesses,
corrections and both outcomes. Do not describe original-source refusals as all
green or infer an application vulnerability solely from these generated states.

The isolated production HTTP preparation also covers schemas with live
read-through. Preview population captures live in the disposable snapshot's
main database: activation may switch SQLite's temp store, which drops TEMP
tables. This preserves the existing before/after comparison; no live release
state or activation semantics change.

Typed payload recognition includes concrete rows already returned by an
incomplete page: incompleteness cannot undo a disclosure. Repeatability and
actual compiler-typed field identity are still required. Paired absence/boolean
comparisons retain their completeness restrictions. Every history absence world,
including a ledger reconstruction, revalidates cross-table final-exists before
comparison. Compact advisory headers make no claim that the whole check passed.
QRY owns changes to partial-return execution and hidden lookup semantics.

Implicit-write stability distinguishes affected tables from possibly deleted
tables. Plain updates do not fire on-delete cascades. Explicit deletes and
expiring written tables may delete; maintained targets and live read-through
retirement conservatively propagate possible deletion. Cascade actions continue
the deletion closure, while set-null propagates writes (and expiry if present).
This permits the upheld-appeal update intent of wave-18 033 without discarding
its appeal predicate merely because bouts declare an unrelated delete cascade.
No runtime deletion or cascade semantics change.

Expiring writes use the runtime's `is_expiring()` classification, including
inherited read-through deadlines and ephemeral lifetimes. Replacing an expired
identity may retire its predecessor and cascade approvals before later guards;
that dependency is never treated as stable just because authored `expire` is absent.


### 13.7 Wave-19 correction: admissible starts and persistent refutations

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-19. This section supersedes conflicting seed-only,
fixture-selection and bounded-check wording above. The coordinator explicitly
commissions this semantic correction after wave-19 day-two evidence.

An absent or row-empty fixture selection uses generated security populations;
an empty directory is not an authored population. Invalid/nonempty explicit
fixtures still refuse. Business-case and saved-source selection retain their
separate meanings. Authored fixture assertions and admitted row-read witnesses
fail even without an authored initialization trace. They are trusted starting
assumptions, not synthetic writes. Arbitrarily invented schema snapshots and
unreachable synthetic writes remain supplemental. Column disclosure still needs
an actual authored observer; raw whole-row admission cannot refute a projected
private field.

Generated candidates are proposals, not trusted fixtures. Bootstrap roster rows
and tables without an authored creator must satisfy an ordinary insert gate for
an identified actor, defaults and all constraints. Where authored creators
exist, generate initial payloads by executing those creators with real typed
arguments and uploaded media, preserving their requires, copied relationships,
constant/default fields and role restrictions. Never independently randomize
values a creator couples. Unsupported creators/populations name their gap;
missing coverage is not proof. Actual later role changes and relationship
transfers retain their committed steps and generated or authored fixture origin.
Historical grants are not silently repaired to the current role/relationship.

The bounded check cannot promise to rediscover every full campaign witness.
`test` records only its concrete refutations and completion metadata, keyed to
all selected source bytes, fixture input bytes and the exact CLI executable.
`check` incorporates matching refutations into its ordinary failing summary;
changed source, fixtures or binary invalidate the record. No cached passing
verdict establishes proof. Persistence failure is explicit. The record lives
with generated project artifacts, not a deployed store or authentication state.

Every escalation note remains visible in short output even beyond the ordinary
five-note cap. Risk names prefer the role conferring the largest established
surface, without relying on names such as admin. Check prints a short exposure
line independent of N_ note rendering. A rule-changing deploy --check explicitly
demands `snapback4 test` when no matching completed campaign exists, including
when no independent invariants are declared. It prints the short exposure
summary; it must not silently imply that changed rules were security-tested.
The demand is actionable preflight output, not a new prohibition on activation.

Negative-existence column policies use actual current snapshot truth, never
negate an unavailable/unknown probe into a refutation. Column update intent
observes only actual changes to that column; changing a sibling is not evidence.
Finite campaign caps and unknown stability boundaries remain explicit. Tests
must cover the wave-19 examples (041 mismatched grant role, 044 assignee,
034 judge/coach overlap, 047 cold-approval state, 019 copied immutable owner),
not suppress findings by weakening independent intent.


Wave-19 review clarifications (controlling): the coordinator explicitly requires
catches from the app's own seed/fixtures to fail again. An authored stale seed
therefore fails under its trusted starting-state assumption; it is never
credited as an observed role-change or transfer. Credit for 019's transition
coverage requires the committed role/relationship mutation in the same trace,
followed by the actual forbidden observer. Generated initial states never gain
that credit merely by arranging stale fields. Partial nonempty authored fixtures
are retained as authored input without silently supplementing invented rows;
row-empty inputs use generation, and missing populations remain named coverage.

Creator discovery uses the bound mutation IR's insert/upsert write census. Its
actual interpreter executes require predicates and copied fields; no symbolic
coupling guess admits a generated payload. Global or explicitly role-bearing
roster proposals and tables without an authored creator require a specific
insert-admitted actor in the pre-insert world; a candidate cannot authorize its
own bootstrap. Text arguments include literals compared in bound binary
expressions (including require role='operator'). Generated starting states are
still explicitly labeled; initialization is never silently called empty-start.

The record covers the fixed full-default security campaign (512 single/pair
history envelope, existing full observer/matrix caps and clock NOW), not a
caller-selected bounded campaign. Exact executable identity binds these
constants and their algorithms; source and sorted fixture names/contents bind
inputs. JSON, verbosity, business-case flags and sweep --as do not change this
security campaign; residency selections do not write records; saved-source tests bind selected
source and contemporary fixture bytes separately from the working source.
Records include attempted/sequence counts and coverage-gap counts. Completion
means all finite security phases returned, not exhaustive coverage or proof;
authentication-boundary failure cannot produce completed evidence. No passing
status is loaded into static proof. Write failure is explicit and leaves the
preview demanding a campaign. Records are generated/security-test.json under
the app's snapback directory; temp-file rename avoids partial reads.

Changed/new/deleted table rules count as rule changes, comparing all four bound
gates against the currently selected release. Preview prints the demand even
with zero invariants; it does not veto actual activation. SEC4 also surfaces
this reminder on ordinary deploy. The short exposure line states table,
conditional-audience and broad-read counts independently of N_ note rendering;
audit retains exact per-role and per-column predicates. Most-privileged naming
sorts candidate roles by the number of table/effect gates proved universally
admitted after substituting that role, descending, then deterministic role
order; the source location, effective gate and recipe remain in the note.
The existing 200-schema false-refusal bar and explicit origin labels remain.

Only failure-eligible origins are persisted or reloaded, with their exact origin
and witness. Supplemental synthetic observations are never promoted by caching.
A later witness-free or interrupted run on identical inputs does not erase
established failures: records merge prior eligible witnesses, deduplicating by
site/observer/viewer/effect/origin; a source, fixture or executable change is
the invalidation boundary. Full phase completion and known violations are
separate metadata. Saved-source deploys cannot borrow the working-source record;
the demand must name the corresponding `test --source <candidate> --fixture ...`
selection, and use matching selected-source evidence, never the working-source record.

Distinct committed operation/viewer paths are not collapsed merely because
their lengths match. A one-step foundUnit witness cannot hide registerStaff,
and a one-step provision cannot hide linkUnit. Deduplication preserves each
operation/viewer path; repeated values on that path remain finite sampling.
A declined duplicate never mutates a previous witness's operation or arguments.
Relationship candidates prioritize actual admitted principal transfers from the
initial world, with explicit bounded probing, followed by ordinary replay and
observation. No preflight-only candidate is itself a refutation.

Retained-grant lifetime advice no longer requires declared role intent when a
grant writer depends on a mutable role roster but the read branch does not
consult that roster. Its wording is conditional: grants can survive role
changes; confirm whether current-role intent is required. This does not infer
a privacy specification or refute an invariant. It restores 019's pointer while
keeping lifetime advice out of the escalation/failing-witness tier.

A bounded check with matching known failures may skip additional concrete
resampling. It still runs static analysis and advice, incorporates the eligible
witnesses, names the skipped coverage and fails. Other obligations stay unproved;
full audit/test retain their campaigns. This avoids paying both executable
fingerprinting and exploratory work merely to rediscover a known failure.

Counterfactual column worlds must also respect authored coupling. A field
produced only by copied/computed/constant writer expressions, or by an argument
that is additionally used in a guard/another expression, is not independently
perturbed. The paired reduction stays uncovered unless a bound writer supplies
it from an independently used typed argument. Actual compiler-typed payloads
and committed transition observations still run and can fail. In particular,
changing immutable reports.homeowner while leaving its source chimney.owner
fixed cannot make a safe self-filtered query appear to leak a private field.

Records bind the bytes actually consumed, not a fresh read after testing. Source
strings are captured before compilation. Selected fixture inputs, including
symlink targets followed by the loader, are copied once into private disposable
scratch and both business/security phases consume that copy. The key is derived
from those captured source/fixture bytes. Before publishing the record, compare
the current original selection to that key; a change is explicit stale-input
evidence and does not persist. Later fixture symlink-target changes invalidate
records exactly like ordinary file edits. Scratch is removed on every return.
# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts and five actionable notes; `--verbose` retains every reason.
A mirror invariant cannot catch a wrong rule: the advisory asks
for the spec's audience, resource relationship and lifecycle condition instead.
A legitimate simple owner policy can be equivalent; the advisory never refuses it.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: it offers unconfirmed
per-role/per-column alternatives and questions, never auto-accepted policy.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

### N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE

N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE means intent and the effective rule admit the same cases
in the bounded predicate kernel. Check the original requirement before rewriting; an equivalent policy
may be correct, but cannot detect a mistake copied into both declarations.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_READ

N_ROLE_WIDE_READ means a role-wide branch gated by a row value exposes every column
on matching rows.
Use a board/projection table for a public rating; declare intent for the private
notes, principals, images and files separately.

`test` uses generation when fixtures are absent or contain no rows, including
an empty starter fixtures directory. Populated authored fixtures stay selected;
seeded read and integrity violations fail under that explicit starting-state
assumption. Generated payloads run authored creation mutations, preserving
requires, immutable copies and recipient-role checks; declined populations are
coverage gaps. A recorded role change or relationship transfer is shown in the
counterexample history; a stale starting snapshot is not such a history.

A completed `test` records concrete refutations under
`snapback/generated/security-test.json`. The next `check` reports matching
failures even when its short campaign cannot rediscover them. Exact source,
fixture bytes and CLI identity bind the record; edits invalidate it. No cached
passing result proves security. Every escalation note stays visible in short
output. Rule-changing deploy previews print exposure counts and demand a test
for the selected source/fixtures when a matching full finite campaign is absent.
# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```
For a complete real-user staging script and first-admin setup, run `guide staging`. Bulk account setup is restricted to fresh `deploy --staging` stores. Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. Signups are rate-limited: create proof accounts sequentially and respect a returned retry delay. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development and classified staging proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`); production always uses wall time.

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity
Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:
Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```
Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness. Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```
```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```
This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads. A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or a declarative `backfill table name(fields: types): {column: expression}`; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused deploy removes its candidate directory and private stage. An ambiguous reply preserves source until committed history resolves the outcome; inspect releases before retrying. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Source identical to the active release is a no-op; source matching a retained release reactivates its existing id with restoration and full validation. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; selecting retained source by deploy or rollback already selects restoration. The same --restore-dormant flag is accepted by both commands. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
```
Previews lead with removed guards and their refusal codes, then group query/count active-table losses, linked dormant rows and safety dependencies per change. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Original read-through captures restore only with the same capture definition; new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback
Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. Required operation parameters remain required; only declared optional, defaulted or deprecated arguments may be omitted. Baseline generation also warns about old-client input breaks and result/refusal changes. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.

`useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/existing-argument signatures differ or the server adds a required argument; extra optional served parameters are compatible. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.

## Trigger a deployed job
A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
```sh
export SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
bunx snapback4 run reconcile --release ./deployment --json
unset SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN
```
`--token-env NAME` reads another environment variable. The command uses served source, so it also works outside the app directory. A lost reply is ambiguous; inspect the queue before retrying. Starter/client recipes: `guide client`.

`guide production` covers real accounts: add signup/login and persistent sessions, replace personas with principals, provision authorized profiles/roles and memberships, wait for scopes before offline reads, and bootstrap without fixtures. Prove role checks, sign-out, restart and rollback on durable storage behind TLS (`guide client`).

## Inspection, proxies and TLS
Dev admits loopback personas or bearer sessions for queries, writes and the compatibility record; serve requires bearer sessions even for public rows. Identity config/sign-in endpoints are public. Seed/dev endpoints refuse in serve; system job doors require full service seats. `guide wire` gives the HTTP contract.
Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).

`snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json` returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. Inspection bypasses read rules and can show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface. Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS. Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity. `--json-objects` returns `{header, columns, rows, limit, more, ruleFiltered:false}` with object `rows`; `--json` uses positional `rows`. Put flags outside the quoted SQL. N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.

## Capture a saved version
After check and business cases pass, from the app root:
```sh
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --save-version versions/v1
snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check --bridge ./bridge
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --check
```
The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete current Quarry source and this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.

## Keep v1 clients working

`deploy --check --json` reports `clientCompatibility` separately from data adoption: missing operations, changed kinds, required new arguments, removed or narrowed inputs, result shape/value changes and changed refusal codes/messages. Deploy prints the same warnings. Input breaks name the old release and the E_INPUT/E_OP outcome. A result or behavior review asks you to verify the intended contract; a correct new privacy denial is intentional behavior, not an input shape failure. The comparison is conservative, not a proof of unchanged logic. For example, save this v1 source and its callers sending `{name:"Ada",obsolete:"old input"}` to `greet`:

```quarry
use identity guests
query greet(name: text <=40, obsolete: text <=40):
  return {message: name + '!'}
```
Deploy this v2 source. The unchanged v1 call still returns `{message:"Ada!"}`; new callers can use `greeting` and omit `suffix` and `obsolete`:

```quarry
use identity guests
query greeting(name: text <=40, suffix: text <=1 default '!', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated):
  return {message: name + suffix}
alias greet = greeting
```
A literal default handles omitted or null arguments and must fit the declared scalar type; explicit non-null input still validates. `deprecated` accepts a validated optional argument but forbids referring to it in the body. Unknown arguments still refuse. These modifiers apply to non-native Quarry queries and mutations; helpers, actions and jobs keep their distinct invocation contracts. Aliases target an authored query/mutation in the same module, carry the same rules/guards/results, and cannot chain. Retain the old endpoint until its callers have retired; preserve the old result projection and handle intended denials.

Deploy also warns when later rollback can refuse or hide rows: dropping a required old column or widening a value domain can strand newly written rows. The preflight separately attempts exact reverse adoption on its post-deploy snapshot, naming present-data failures. Keep old fields and compatible domains in an expand/contract bridge; after writes run the named `rollback <deployment> --to N --check --bridge <new-dir>` before selecting N. A green snapshot never freezes later writes.

## Persistent production: v1 → v2 → rollback → forward

Replacement transforms read reversible preimages; input types must cover original and native rows. Re-entry preserves native outputs with unchanged inputs; otherwise use a bridge.

Save this script and run it with Node. `PRODUCTION_DIR` selects its persistent store; reruns preserve rows/users. It proves backfill, privacy and rollback/forward behavior. Rollback restores v1's broader reads; bridge if inappropriate. REL5 owns reactivation/old-client reporting; SEC3 owns privacy/audit.

```js
// day-two-production.mjs — this exact fence runs against the packed package.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve } from "node:path";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { cli, deploy, rollback, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
import { signup } from "snapback4/auth";

const directory = resolve(process.env.PRODUCTION_DIR ?? "production-day-two");
const v1 = `use identity password
table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('member', 'reviewer')
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('member', 'reviewer')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] {principal, role}
table tickets:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  weight: int
  length: int
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'reviewer']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation add(body: text 1..200, weight: int, length: int):
  return insert tickets {owner:viewer, body, weight, length}
mutation revise(ticket: tickets, weight: int):
  update tickets[ticket] {weight}
query ticket(ticket: tickets):
  return tickets[ticket]
`;
const v2 = v1
  .replace("  owner: principal", "  vehicleClass: enum('car', 'van', 'commercial')\n  owner: principal")
  .replace("  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'reviewer']", "  read <- .owner = viewer")
  .replace("{owner:viewer, body, weight, length}", "{owner:viewer, body, weight, length, vehicleClass:'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car'}")
  + `backfill tickets vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  {vehicleClass:'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car'}
-- Spec: only the ticket owner may revise its weight.
invariant tickets.revise only .owner = viewer
`;
for (const [version, source] of [["v1", v1], ["v2", v2]]) {
  mkdirSync(`versions/${version}`, { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(`versions/${version}/schema.q`, source);
}
async function checked(args) {
  const result = await cli(args);
  assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr + result.stdout);
  return result.stdout;
}
async function snapshot() {
  const count = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets", "--release", directory, "--json"])).rows[0][0];
  const full = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT id, owner, body, weight, length FROM tickets ORDER BY id", "--release", directory, "--limit", String(Math.max(1, count + 1)), "--json"]));
  assert.equal(full.rows.length, count, "complete population");
  return new Map(full.rows.map(row => [row[0], row]));
}
if (existsSync(directory + "/store.sqlite")) await rollback(directory, 1);
else await deploy(directory, "versions/v1");
const host = await startServe(directory);
try {
  const before = await snapshot();
  const users = [];
  for (const role of ["member", "reviewer"]) {
    const signed = await signup(host.url, `${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, randomUUID() + randomUUID());
    assert.equal(signed.ok, true);
    users.push(signed.session);
  }
  const [alice, reviewer] = users;
  async function post(path, session, args) {
    const response = await fetch(host.url + path, { method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json", authorization:`Bearer ${session.token}`}, body:JSON.stringify({args}), signal:AbortSignal.timeout(10000) });
    assert.equal(response.status, 200);
    return response.json();
  }
  const operator = {token:await host.operatorToken()};
  for (const [index, role] of ["member", "reviewer"].entries()) {
    assert.equal((await post("/m/provision", operator, {principal:users[index].principal, role})).state, "sent");
  }
  const first = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"historical ticket", weight:1000, length:400});
  assert.equal(first.state, "sent");
  const ticket = first.result.id;
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data.body, "historical ticket", "v1 reviewer can read");
  const historical = await snapshot(); assert.equal(historical.size, before.size + 1);
  const preview = await checked(["deploy", directory, "--schema", "versions/v2", "--check", "--restore-dormant"]);
  assert.match(preview, /vehicleClass/);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "read-only preview");
  await deploy(directory, "versions/v2", {args:["--restore-dormant"]});
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "historical fields preserved");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "car");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "v2 tightened privacy");
  const oldCall = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"old-shaped call on v2", weight:4000, length:400});
  assert.equal(oldCall.state, "sent"); assert.equal(oldCall.result.vehicleClass, "commercial");
  const allV2 = await snapshot(); assert.equal(allV2.size, historical.size + 1);
  await rollback(directory, 1);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), allV2, "native rows preserved");
  const oldView = (await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data;
  assert.equal(oldView.body, "historical ticket"); assert.ok(!("vehicleClass" in oldView));
  assert.equal((await post("/m/revise", alice, {ticket, weight:4000})).state, "sent");
  const changed = await snapshot();
  const expected = new Map(allV2); const changedRow = [...expected.get(ticket)]; changedRow[3] = 4000; expected.set(ticket, changedRow);
  assert.deepEqual(changed, expected, "only intended edit");
  await rollback(directory, "latest");
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), changed, "complete population preserved");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "commercial", "old edit caught up");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "privacy restored");
  console.log(JSON.stringify({status:"passed", persistent:true, before:before.size, after:changed.size, backfill:"car → commercial after v1 edit", privacy:"v1 broad → v2 private → v1 broad → v2 private"}));
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```

Storage, users and rows survive. Resolve reported conflicts through a truthful bridge.

When table rules change, deploy and deploy --check print a short security
exposure summary. Without a matching completed security campaign they demand
`snapback4 test` for the selected source and fixtures (saved-source previews name
`test --source`). Known refutations and coverage gaps remain visible; a finite
campaign is not proof. This reminder does not change activation authority.

I’m using the read-only `llp-orient` workflow to identify the governing constraints. Section 13.7 controls this review; I’ll inspect how generated starts establish reachability and how persisted evidence binds to the inputs actually tested.
