   173	fn check_change(
   174	    dir: &Path,
   175	    previous: Option<u64>,
   176	    bound: &snapback4_lang::Bound,
   177	    options: &Options,
   178	    target_release: Option<u64>,
   179	) -> Result<(), String> {
   180	    if !options.flags.contains_key("check") && let Some(n) = previous {
   181	        let old = bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(n.to_string()))?;
   182	        let store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite")).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   183	        let target_label = target_release.map(|n| format!("release {n}")).unwrap_or_else(|| "the new release".into());
   184	        let lines = super::release_narration::compatibility(&store, &old.schema, &bound.schema, n, &target_label, target_release.is_none())?;
   185	        for line in lines {
   186	            eprintln!("{line}");
   187	        }
   188	        if target_release.is_some() {
   189	            for warning in snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compare(&old,bound,&format!("release {n}")) {eprintln!("WARNING {warning}");}
   190	        }
   191	    }
   192	    if options.flags.contains_key("allow-destructive") {
   193	        eprintln!(
   194	            "deploy/rollback retain storage; --allow-destructive does not bypass validation or prune. To drop dormant storage: snapback4 prune {} --allow-destructive",
   195	            dir.display()
   196	        );
   197	    }
   198	    Ok(())
   199	}
   200	
   201	fn command_lock(dir: &Path) -> Result<std::fs::File, String> {
   202	    #[cfg(unix)]
   203	    {
   204	        let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(
   205	            &dir.join("store.sqlite"),
   206	            &dir.join(".release-command"),
   207	            true,
   208	            0,
   209	        )
   210	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   211	        file.lock().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   212	        Ok(file)
   213	    }
   214	    #[cfg(not(unix))]
   215	    Err("release commands require Unix".into())
   216	}
   217	fn target(dir: &Path) -> Result<Option<Owner>, String> {
   218	    match deployment_lock(dir) {
   219	        Ok(owner) => Ok(Some(owner)),
   220	        Err(error) if error.contains("E_STORE_BUSY") => Ok(None),
   221	        Err(error) => Err(error),
   222	    }
   223	}
   224	fn status(
   225	    dir: &Path,
   226	    owner: Option<&Owner>,
   227	    after: u64,
   228	    items_after: Option<String>,
   229	    event: Option<u64>,
   230	) -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
   231	    match owner {
   232	        Some(owner) => owner
   233	            .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
   234	            .and_then(|mut s| s.release_status(after, items_after.as_deref(), event))
   235	            .map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
   236	        None => crate::release_control::request(
   237	            dir,
   238	            crate::release_control::Command::Status {
   239	                after,
   240	                items_after,
   241	                event,
   242	            },
   243	        ),
   244	    }
   245	}
   246	fn activate(
   247	    dir: &Path,
   248	    owner: Option<&Owner>,
   249	    release: u64,
   250	    previous: Option<u64>,
   251	    revision: u64,
   252	    bound: snapback4_lang::Bound,
   253	    rollback: bool,
   254	    restore_dormant: bool,
   255	    allow_feed_adoption: bool,
   256	    options: &Options,
   257	    mut candidate: Option<&mut crate::release_files::Candidate>,
   258	) -> Result<(), String> {
   259	    let root = dir.join("releases").join(release.to_string());
   260	    let digest = crate::release_files::digest(&root)?;
   261	    if let Some(owner) = owner {
   262	        let project = Project { root };
   263	        let prepared = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   264	        crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project, &bound.schema)?;
   265	        crate::release_files::link_store(&project.root)?;
   266	        crate::release_files::durable(&project.root)?;
   267	        let mut pointer = crate::release_files::Pointer::prepare(dir, release, previous, rollback)?;
   268	        let activation = snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation {
   269	            release,
   270	            expected_previous: previous,
   271	            expected_revision: Some(revision),
   272	            restore_dormant,
   273	            native_digest: prepared.installed.as_ref().map(|m| m.digest.clone()),
   274	            operation: if rollback { "rollback" } else { "deploy" }.into(),
   275	        };
   276	        let store = owner
   277	            .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
   278	            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   279	        let engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared(
   280	            store,
   281	            bound.schema,
   282	            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
   283	            &activation,
   284	            allow_feed_adoption,
   285	        )
   286	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   287	        pointer.finish(engine.generation())?;
   288	        receipt(release,engine.generation(),&engine.store().adoption_report,options);
   289	
   290	    } else {
   291	        let result = crate::release_control::request_observed(
   292	            dir,
   293	            crate::release_control::Command::Activate {
   294	                release,
   295	                expected_release: previous,
   296	                expected_revision: revision,
   297	                digest,
   298	                rollback,
   299	                restore_dormant,
   300	                allow_feed_adoption,
   301	            },
   302	            |pending| { if let Some(candidate)=candidate.as_deref_mut() {candidate.pending=pending;} },
   303	        )?;
   304	        if options.json {println!("{}",result);} else {
   305	            println!("release {release} is current as generation {}; {}; running serve adopted the release",result["generation"],result["adoption"].as_str().unwrap_or("adopted"));
   306	            for fill in result["backfills"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().filter_map(|f|f.as_str()) {println!("  backfill: {fill}");}
   307	            if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {println!("{}",result["details"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}
   308	        }
   309	    }
   310	    Ok(())
   311	}
   312	fn report(verb: &str, result: Result<(), String>) -> i32 {
   313	    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — release completion owns no engine/lease; bound the final diagnostic drain.
   314	    // The background writer must get a chance to emit adoption estimates before
   315	    // this short-lived command exits. Broken/stalled output cannot hold exit.
   316	    let _ = snapback4_core::diagnostics::drain(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
   317	    match result {
   318	        Ok(()) => 0,
   319	        Err(error) => {
   320	            eprintln!("{verb}: {error}");
   321	            1
   322	        }
   323	    }
   324	}
   325	
   326	pub fn deploy(options: &Options) -> i32 {
   327	    report(
   328	        "deploy",
   329	        (|| {
   330	            let dir = canonical_directory(options)?;
   331	            let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
   332	            let existed = dir.join("store.sqlite").exists();
   333	            let checking=options.flags.contains_key("check");
   334	            let _deployment = if checking {None} else {target(&dir)?};
   335	            if let Some(owner) = &_deployment { owner.report_reclaim(options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
   336	            let project = super::proof_source::project(options, "schema")?;
   337	            let sources = super::proof_source::sources(&project, options, "schema")?;
   338	            let bound = compiled_or_exit(super::proof_source::compile(&sources, options, "schema"), options.json);
   339	            let mut saved_version = super::saved_version::SavedVersion::prepare(&project,&sources,options,&dir)?;
   340	            if options.flags.contains_key("staging") {
   341	                staging::classify(&dir, _deployment.as_ref(), existed, checking)?;
   342	            }
   343	            std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("releases")).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   344	            let held = if checking {
   345	                if dir.join("store.sqlite").exists() {snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite")).and_then(|mut s|s.release_status(0,None,None)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?}
   346	                else {serde_json::json!({"release":null,"revision":0,"generation":1})}
   347	            } else {status(&dir, _deployment.as_ref(), 0, None, None)?};
   348	            let previous = if let Some(owner) = _deployment.as_ref() {
   349	                current(&dir, owner)?
   350	            } else {
   351	                held["release"].as_u64()
   352	            };
   353	            {
   354	                let prior=previous.map(|n|bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(n.to_string()))).transpose()?;
   355	                super::security::release_loop(&project,&bound,prior.as_ref(),options);
   356	            }
   357	            let revision = held["revision"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
   358	            check_change(&dir, previous, &bound, options, None)?;
   359	            let releases = dir.join("releases").canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   360	            let stage = releases.join(format!(".stage-{}", std::process::id()));
   361	            if stage.exists() {std::fs::remove_dir_all(&stage).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;}
   362	            std::fs::create_dir(&stage).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   363	            let _cleanup=Stage(stage.clone());
   364	            std::fs::create_dir(stage.join("snapback")).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   365	            let source_hash = {
   366	                use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
   367	                let mut hash = Sha256::new();
   368	                let mut ordered = sources.iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
   369	                ordered.sort_by(|a,b| a.0.cmp(&b.0));
   370	                for (name,text) in ordered {
   371	                    hash.update((name.len() as u64).to_le_bytes()); hash.update(name.as_bytes());
   372	                    hash.update((text.len() as u64).to_le_bytes()); hash.update(text.as_bytes());
   373	                }
   374	                format!("{:x}", hash.finalize())
   375	            };
   376	            for (name,text) in sources {
   377	                let path=stage.join("snapback").join(name);
   378	                if let Some(parent)=path.parent(){std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;}
   379	                std::fs::write(path,text).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
   380	            }
   381	            crate::dev_worker::package(&project, &stage)?;
   382	            // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#2 — saved schemas prepare companion types in a private snapshot, never in working generated/.
   383	            let private = if options.flags.contains_key("schema") || crate::dev_worker::source(&project).is_some() {
   384	                Some(super::proof_source::Candidate::prepare(&project, &stage, &bound)?)
   385	            } else { None };
   290	}
   291	
   292	pub fn check(options: &Options) -> i32 {
   293	    if options.flags.contains_key("audit") { return security::audit(options); }
   294	    if options.flags.contains_key("store") && options.flags.contains_key("release") {
   295	        return releases::check_release(options);
   296	    }
   297	
   298	    if let Some(file) = options.flags.get("emit-lean") {
   299	        return emit_lean(file);
   300	    }
   301	    let project = project_or_exit();
   302	    let started = Instant::now();
   303	    package_versions::warn(&project);
   304	    upgrade_history::report(&project, options.json);
   305	    if options.flags.contains_key("fix") {
   306	        if let Err(code) = fix::checked(fix::compile(&project)) { return code; }
   307	    }
   308	    let compiled_sources = match project.sources() {
   309	        Ok(sources) => sources,
   310	        Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot read snapback/: {error}"); return 2; }
   311	    };
   312	    let mut bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&compiled_sources);
   313	    let reviewable_notes = bound.notes.iter().map(note_reviews::fingerprint).collect();
   314	    for warning in saved_fixture_drift::warnings(&project) {
   315	        if warning.drift { eprintln!("check: WARNING: saved fixture drift: {}", warning.message); }
   316	        else { eprintln!("check: info: {}", warning.message); }
   317	    }
   318	    if options.flags.contains_key("fix") && bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   319	        if let Err(error) = fix_fixtures::run(&project, &bound, options) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("check --fix: {error}")); return 2; }
   320	    }
   321	
   322	    if let Some(hint) = project.saved_copy_hint() { if options.json { eprintln!("check: {hint}"); } else { println!("check: {hint}"); } }
   323	    if let Some(error) = crate::exact_device::inspect(&project.root, &bound.schema).into_iter().find_map(Result::err) {
   324	        if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "denied": {"code": "E_DEVICE_VERSION", "message": error}, "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
   325	        else { eprintln!("{error}"); }
   326	        return 1;
   327	    }
   328	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   329	        check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json);
   330	        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   331	        if !options.json { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
   332	        return 1;
   333	    }
   334	    let security = security::report(&project, &bound, options);
   335	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.3 — check judges adoption against the dev store's active generation and rows, read-only
   336	    match adoption::diagnostics(&project, &bound.schema, options.flags.get("store"), options.json || options.flags.contains_key("verbose")) {
   337	        Ok((problems, notes)) => { bound.diagnostics.extend(problems); bound.notes.extend(notes); },
   338	        Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot check store adoption: {error}")); return 2; }
   339	    }
   340	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   341	        check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json);
   342	        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   343	        if !options.json { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
   344	        return 1;
   345	    }
   346	    for diagnostic in readiness::notes(&project, &bound, options.flags.get("fixture")) {
   347	        if diagnostic.code == "E_FIXTURE_TIME" { bound.diagnostics.push(diagnostic); }
   348	        else { bound.notes.push(diagnostic); }
   349	    }
   350	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   351	        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   352	        return 1;
   353	    }
   354	    bound.notes.extend(snapback4_lang::bind::residency::change_notes(&bound, &residency::previous(&project)));
   355	    check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json);
   356	    if let Err(error) = project.write_generated(&bound) {
   357	        failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write generated types: {error}"));
   358	        return 2;
   359	    }
   360	    if let Err(error) = project.check_server() {
   361	        if options.json { println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": if error.starts_with("E_SERVER_TOOLCHAIN") { "E_SERVER_TOOLCHAIN" } else { "E_SERVER_TYPECHECK" }, "message": error, "guide": "actions" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] })); }
   362	        else { eprintln!("{error}"); }
   363	        return 1;
   364	    }
   365	    // A native job's module is built here too (LLP 3000.005): the schema's
   366	    // Rust types written, cargo run, the module checked against the jobs.
   367	    let native = match crate::native::build(&project, &bound) {
   368	        Ok(built) => built,
   369	        Err(problem) => {
   370	            if options.json {
   371	                println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": problem.code, "message": problem.message, "rewrite": problem.rewrite, "guide": "native" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] }));
   372	            } else {
   373	                eprintln!("{problem}\n");
   374	                eprintln!("check: 1 problem");
   375	            }
   376	            return 1;
   377	        }
   378	    };
   379	    check_surface::remember(&project, &bound);
   380	    if let Err(error) = residency::remember(&project, &bound) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write residency facts: {error}")); return 2; }
   381	    let acquisition = if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
   382	        match residency::at_check(&project, &bound, options) {
   383	            Ok(report) => report,
   384	            Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("residency measurement failed: {error}")); return 1; }
   385	        }
   386	    } else { None };
   387	    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#132-reviewed-notes — every failed check preserves review evidence, including independent policy refutations.
   388	    let reviewed = if security.refuted() {
   389	        note_reviews::Review { new: bound.notes.clone(), acknowledged: Vec::new() }
   390	    } else { match note_reviews::review(&project, &compiled_sources, &bound.notes, &reviewable_notes, options.flags.contains_key("ack-notes")) {
   391	        Ok(review) => review,
   392	        Err(error) => {
   393	            if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "problems": [{"code": "E_CHECK_NOTES", "message": error}], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
   394	            else { eprintln!("check: E_CHECK_NOTES: {error}"); }
   395	            return 2;
   396	        }
   397	    } };
   398	    let present = |text: &str| crate::presentation::indexes(&bound.schema, text, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   399	    let details = acquisition.is_none() || options.flags.contains_key("verbose");
   400	    let certificates: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().map(|p| crate::scale::analyze_bound(&bound.schema, p)).collect();
   401	    let scale_summary = crate::scale::summary(&certificates);
   402	    let note_count = security.notes.len() + reviewed.new.len() + certificates.iter().map(|certificate| certificate.search.len()).sum::<usize>();
   403	    let field_authority: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name,table)| table.written_by.iter().map(move |(column,authority)|
   404	        format!("{name}.{column}: written by .{}; visibility: {}", authority.owner, if authority.audience { "readable by audience" } else { "private to owner" }))).collect();
   405	    let row_views = bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter_map(|m| m.row_key.as_ref().map(|k| json!({
   406	        "view": m.target, "source": m.source, "sourceKey": k.source_key, "member": k.table,
   407	        "memberKey": k.member_key, "fanout": k.fanout, "range": row_view_range(&bound.schema, m), "writerRecount": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::WRITER_RECOUNT, "recountChunk": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::RECOUNT_CHUNK
   408	    }))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   409	    let elapsed = started.elapsed();
   410	    let synced = bound.schema.synced().count();
   411	    let sequences: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name, table)| table.sequences.iter().map(move |(column, reference)| {
   412	        format!("sequence {name}.{column}: counter per {} row through .{reference}; immutable, provisional while pending", table.columns[reference])
   413	    })).collect();
   414	    if options.json {
   415	        println!("{}", json!({ "ok": !security.refuted(), "security": &security, "followingHistory": bound.programs.iter().filter_map(|p| crate::scale::following::certificate(&bound.schema, &p.program)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "rowViews": row_views, "fieldAuthority": field_authority, "sequences": sequences, "scaleSummary": scale_summary, "search": certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "plans": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(bound.programs.iter().map(|p| (&p.program.name, p.selections())).collect::<BTreeMap<_, _>>()) } else { None }, "scale": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(&certificates) } else { None }, "tables": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).count(), "views": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some()).count(), "synced": synced, "operations": bound.programs.len(), "heldQueries": bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, fact)| fact.held).map(|(name, _)| name).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": reviewed.new, "acknowledgedNotes": reviewed.acknowledged, "predictionSummary": snapback4_lang::bind::residency::prediction_summary(&bound), "starterLeftovers": init::starter_leftovers_json(&project.root), "acquisition": acquisition, "residency": if options.flags.contains_key("residency") { Some(residency::facts(&bound)) } else { None }, "native": native.as_ref().map(|b| json!({ "module": b.path, "bytes": b.bytes, "jobs": b.jobs, "ms": b.ms })), "ms": elapsed.as_millis() }));
   416	    } else {
   417	        println!("check: {} — {} ({synced} synced), {} operations, {}{}, {:.1} ms{}", if security.refuted() { "failed" } else { "ok" }, table_counts(&bound.schema), bound.programs.len(), if security.refuted() { "security invariant refuted" } else { "no problems" }, if note_count == 0 { String::new() } else { format!(", {} note{}", note_count, if note_count == 1 { "" } else { "s" }) }, elapsed.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0, match &native { Some(b) => format!("; native: {} → {} ({} B, {} ms)", b.jobs.join(", "), b.path.display(), b.bytes, b.ms), None => String::new() });
   418	        if !reviewed.acknowledged.is_empty() { println!("{} reviewed notes (check --verbose to inspect); source edits require review again", reviewed.acknowledged.len()); }
   419	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
   420	            for line in &sequences { println!("  {line}"); }
   421	            for (name, _) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_, t)| t.ephemeral.is_some()) {
   422	                println!("  {name}: {}", snapback4_lang::bind::residency::table_line(&bound.schema, name));
   423	            }
   424	            for (name, fact) in bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, f)| f.ephemeral) {
   425	                println!("{}", present(&snapback4_lang::bind::residency::operation_lines(name, fact)));
   426	            }
   427	        }
   428	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") { for line in &field_authority { println!("  {line}"); } }
   429	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
   430	            // Verbose keeps every explanation, in the same consequence order as the summary.
   431	            let mut ranked: Vec<&snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = bound.notes.iter().collect();
   432	            ranked.sort_by_key(|note| crate::note_rank::tier(&note.code));
   433	            let ranked: Vec<snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = ranked.into_iter().cloned().collect();
   434	            for report in note_reports(&ranked, true) { println!("{}", present(&report)); }
   435	        } else {
   436	            // Notes are advisory: ranked and grouped after the ok line, repeats shortened (note_rank).
   437	            let mut notes = reviewed.new.iter().chain(&security.notes).map(|note| {
   438	                let compact = completeness_note(note);
   439	                crate::note_rank::Note { site: note.site.to_string(), code: note.code.clone(), summary: present(&note_summary(note)),
   440	                    form: compact.is_none().then(|| crate::note_rank::compiled_form(&note.code, &note.rewrite)).flatten().map(|(lead, code)| (lead, present(&code))), compact }
   441	            })
   442	                .chain(certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).map(|search| crate::note_rank::Note { site: search.site.to_string(), code: search.code.to_string(), summary: present(search.summary), form: None, compact: None }))
   443	                .collect::<Vec<_>>();
   444	            notes.sort_by_key(|note|(crate::note_rank::summary_order(&note.code),note.site.clone(),note.code.clone(),note.summary.clone()));
   445	            let cap=5.max(notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").count());
   446	            let remaining=notes.len().saturating_sub(cap);notes.truncate(cap);
   447	            if remaining>0 {println!("{remaining} more actionable notes in check --verbose/JSON");}
   448	            for line in crate::note_rank::render(notes) { println!("{}", crate::presentation::abbreviate_quoted(&line)); }
   449	        }
   450	    }
//! Role authority is compared by effective gates, never by names like 'admin'.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#123-actionable-authority-advice — advisory grant-edge analysis, not a security proof or implicit exemption.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn probe_pairs(bound:&Bound,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[RuleTerm],equalities:&[(String,RuleTerm)])->Vec<(String,RuleTerm)>{
    let mut pairs=equalities.to_vec();
    if let Some(idx)=bound.schema.table(table).and_then(|t|t.index(index)){for (component,term) in idx.components.iter().zip(key){if let Some(column)=component.column(){pairs.push((column.into(),term.clone()));}}}
    pairs
}
pub(super) fn global_principal(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->Option<String>{
    let t=bound.schema.table(table)?;
    t.indexes.values().find_map(|index|{
        if !index.unique||index.components.len()!=1{return None;}
        let column=index.components[0].column()?;
        matches!(t.columns.get(column),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).then(||column.to_string())
    })
}
pub(super) fn membership_probe(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{
    let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=rule else{return false;};
    if table!=roster{return false;}
    let Some(principal)=global_principal(bound,table)else{return false;};
    let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    pairs.len()==1&&pairs[0].0==principal&&pairs[0].1==RuleTerm::Viewer
}
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — ownership is required for every supplied owned field;
// only an exclusively owned patch bypasses an unnamed row update gate.
fn write_admission(table:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,op:ast::WriteOp,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->RuleExpr{
    let touched=fields.iter().filter(|f|!f.spread&&f.name!="id").map(|f|f.name.as_str()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let owned_only=!touched.is_empty()&&!fields.iter().any(|f|f.spread)&&touched.iter().all(|f|table.written_by.contains_key(*f));
    let gate=match op{
        ast::WriteOp::Insert=>table.rules.insert.clone(),
        ast::WriteOp::Update if owned_only&&!table.update_transition=>RuleExpr::Allow,
        ast::WriteOp::Update=>table.update_rule_judged().into_owned(),
        _=>RuleExpr::Deny,
    };
    let mut owners=table.written_by.iter().filter(|(field,authority)|touched.contains(field.as_str())||op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&!authority.audience&&table.rules.update.needs_field_value(field,&table.columns[*field])).map(|(_,authority)|RuleExpr::Eq(if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{RuleTerm::Field(authority.owner.clone())}else{RuleTerm::OldField(authority.owner.clone())},RuleTerm::Viewer)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    owners.push(RuleExpr::Or(gate.disjuncts().iter().filter(|r|r.service_grant().is_none()).map(|r|(*r).clone()).collect()));
    RuleExpr::And(owners)
}
pub(super) fn membership_grant_risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,operation:&str,requirements:&[RuleExpr],op:ast::WriteOp,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->bool{
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Insert,fields)||membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Update,fields);}
    fn honors(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{match rule{
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster)),
        r=>membership_probe(bound,r,roster),
    }}
    if global_principal(bound,roster).is_none()||!bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster))){return false;}
    let table=&bound.schema.tables[roster];
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(write_admission(table,op,fields)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&global_principal(bound,roster).is_some_and(|column|kernel::implies(&admission,&RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(column.clone()),RuleTerm::Field(column)))==Ok(true)){return false;}
    let ordinary=substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster","$membership",None,None);
    bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.is_none()&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).any(|p|super::operation::safe(p)&&kernel::implies(&ordinary,p)==Ok(false))
}
fn substitute(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,role:Option<&str>,target:Option<&str>)->RuleExpr{
    let known=|t:&RuleTerm|match t{RuleTerm::Field(c)|RuleTerm::NextField(c) if c==column=>target.map(|s|snapback4_core::Value::String(s.into())),RuleTerm::Literal(v)=>Some(v.clone()),_=>None};
    match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,key,index,equalities,..} if table==roster&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>{
            let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
            let requested=pairs.iter().find(|(c,_)|c==column).and_then(|(_,t)|if let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{Some(s.as_str())}else{None});
            if requested.is_none(){rule.clone()}else if requested==role{
                if pairs.iter().all(|(c,t)|c==column||matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{rule.clone()}
            }else{RuleExpr::Deny}
        },
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)),RuleTerm::Viewer) if seat.starts_with("service:")=>RuleExpr::Deny,
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)=>if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(known(a),known(b)){if (a==b)==matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Eq(..)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{RuleExpr::Deny}}else{rule.clone()},
        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
        RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>RuleExpr::Or(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
        RuleExpr::Not(r)=>RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target))),
        _=>rule.clone(),
    }
}
pub(super) fn risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str,operation:&str,targets:&[String],op:ast::WriteOp,requirements:&[RuleExpr],dynamic:bool,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->Option<String>{
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Insert,requirements,dynamic,fields).or_else(||risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Update,requirements,dynamic,fields));}
    let table=bound.schema.table(roster)?;
    if requirements.iter().any(|r|!r.disjuncts().is_empty()&&r.disjuncts().iter().all(|b|b.service_grant().is_some())){return None;}
    let roles=role_values(bound,roster,column);
    let intents=bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c|c==column)&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    fn role_guards(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,out:&mut Vec<RuleExpr>){match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))&&probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|role_columns(bound,table).contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_))))=>out.push(rule.clone()),
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{role_guards(bound,r,out)},_=>{}
    }}
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(write_admission(table,op,fields)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&kernel::implies(&admission,&RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(column.into()),RuleTerm::Field(column.into())))==Ok(true){return None;}
    let mut guards=Vec::new();role_guards(bound,&admission,&mut guards);
    let independent=kernel::implies(&substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster",column,None,None),&RuleExpr::Or(guards))==Ok(true);
    let mut ranked=roles.iter().filter(|r|targets.is_empty()||targets.contains(r)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let score=|role:&&String|bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(_,t)|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]).filter(|rule|kernel::implies(&RuleExpr::Allow,&substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,Some(role.as_str()),None))==Ok(true)).count();
    ranked.sort_by_key(|role|(std::cmp::Reverse(score(role)),role.as_str()));
    for target in ranked{
        for caller in std::iter::once(None).chain(roles.iter().map(|s|Some(s.as_str()))){
            if caller==Some(target.as_str()){continue;}
            let admitted=substitute(bound,&admission,roster,column,caller,Some(target));
            let admits=kernel::implies(&admitted,&RuleExpr::Deny)!=Ok(true);
            if !admits{continue;}
            let violates_intent=intents.iter().any(|p|kernel::implies(&admitted,&substitute(bound,p,roster,column,caller,Some(target)))==Ok(false));
            // Existing independently gated appointers may delegate different
            // roles. Independent intent can restrict a particular target role.
            if independent&&!violates_intent{continue;}
            if violates_intent{return Some(format!("{roster}.{column} assignment to '{target}' is not established by the independent delegation intent for {}",caller.unwrap_or("a caller without a role")));}
            for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (effect,rule) in [("read",&t.rules.read),("insert",&t.rules.insert),("update",&t.rules.update),("delete",&t.rules.delete)]{
                let granted=substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,Some(target),None);let previous=substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,caller,None);
                if kernel::implies(&RuleExpr::Allow,&granted)!=Ok(true){continue;}
                if !dynamic&&!(matches!(effect,"read"|"delete")||!role_columns(bound,name).is_empty()){continue;}
                if kernel::implies(&granted,&previous)==Ok(false){return Some(format!("{} can confer '{target}', which adds {name}.{effect} authority not held by {}",roster,caller.unwrap_or("a caller without a role")));}
            }}
        }
    }None
}

pub(super) fn role_columns(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->BTreeSet<String>{
    let mut columns=BTreeSet::new();
    if let Some(t)=bound.schema.table(table){for c in ["role","kind"]{if t.columns.contains_key(c){columns.insert(c.into());}}}
    fn collect(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,table:&str,columns:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table:t,index,key,equalities,..} if t==table&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>for (c,v) in &probe_pairs(bound,t,index,key,equalities){if matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_))){columns.insert(c.clone());}},
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{collect(bound,r,table,columns)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>collect(bound,r,table,columns),_=>{}
    }}
    let named=columns.clone();let mut observed=BTreeSet::new();
    for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,table,&mut observed);}}
    let global=bound.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t|t.indexes.values().any(|index|{
        if !index.unique{return false;}
        let Some(parts)=index.components.iter().map(|p|p.column()).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()else{return false;};
        let principals=parts.iter().filter(|c|matches!(t.columns.get(**c).map(|k|k.required()),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal))).count();
        if principals!=1{return false;}
        parts.iter().all(|c|matches!(t.columns.get(*c).map(|k|k.required()),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal))||named.contains(*c))
            ||named.is_empty()&&observed.len()==1&&parts.len()==2&&parts.iter().all(|c|observed.contains(*c)||matches!(t.columns.get(*c).map(|k|k.required()),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)))
    }));
    if global{for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,table,&mut columns);}}}else{columns.clear();}
    columns
}

pub(super) fn role_values(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str)->Vec<String>{
    let Some(table)=bound.schema.table(roster)else{return Vec::new();};
    let Some(kind)=table.columns.get(column)else{return Vec::new();};
    match kind.required(){snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum{variants}=>variants.clone(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Text{..}=>{
        let mut roles=BTreeSet::new();
        fn collect(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,roles:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
            RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if table==roster=>for (c,t) in &probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities){if c==column&&let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{roles.insert(s.clone());}},
            RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles),_=>{}
        }}
        for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,roster,column,&mut roles);}}roles.into_iter().collect()
    },_=>Vec::new()}
}
//! Expanded helpers can write from any expression block.
// @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — legacy eligibility examines the complete expanded write footprint.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn stmts_write_tables(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    for stmt in stmts {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Insert { table, .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "insert")),
            Stmt::Update { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "update")),
            Stmt::Upsert { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "upsert")),
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, upsert, .. } => out.push((target.table.clone(), if *upsert { "upsert" } else { "update" })),
            Stmt::Delete { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "delete")),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => out.push((target.table.clone(), "delete")),
            _ => {}
        }
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Push { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr_write_tables(value, out),
            Stmt::Emit { payload: value, once, .. } => { expr_write_tables(value, out); if let Some(value) = once { expr_write_tables(value, out); } },
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                stmts_write_tables(then_body, out);
                stmts_write_tables(else_body, out);
            }
            Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => {
                match domain {
                    LoopDomain::Page(scan) => scan_write_tables(scan, out),
                    LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => { expr_write_tables(start, out); expr_write_tables(end_exclusive, out); }
                    LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => expr_write_tables(value, out),
                    LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => {
                        expr_write_tables(&recurse.seed, out);
                    }
                    LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } | LoopDomain::ArgList { .. } => {}
                }
                stmts_write_tables(body, out);
            }
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr_write_tables(condition, out),
            Stmt::Check { condition, current } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                for key in &current.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { row, .. } | Stmt::Insert { row, .. } => expr_write_tables(row, out),
            Stmt::Update { key, patch, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
            }
            Stmt::Upsert { key, row, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(row, out);
            }
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, condition, .. } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
                if let Some(condition) = condition { stmts_write_tables(&condition.setup, out); expr_write_tables(&condition.value, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Delete { key, .. } => expr_write_tables(key, out),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Break | Stmt::Continue => {}
        }
    }
}

fn scan_write_tables(scan: &Scan, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    if let Some(filter) = &scan.filter { expr_write_tables(filter, out); }
    if let Some(range) = &scan.range {
        for bound in [&range.gt, &range.gte, &range.lt, &range.lte].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(bound, out); }
    }
    for key in &scan.prefix {
        match key { IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) | IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(expr) => expr_write_tables(expr, out) }
    }
    for cursor in [&scan.after, &scan.before].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(cursor, out); }
}

fn expr_write_tables(expr: &Expr, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    match expr {

### 12.7 Stable judgments and direct payload witnesses

Unknown bound policy dependencies never authorize a mutation observer: such
column mutations require disclosure-boundary tracing. Pre-write assertions use
a transitive closure of maintained and cascade/set-null writes; a policy
dependency or target that may change implicitly makes the assertion unsupported.
The same rule covers insert, update, upsert and delete. Looped/opaque net images
cannot stand in for executed write events. Effective inserted authority includes
a defaulted role even when omitted from the source write.

An authored complete query returning a compiler-typed Row/Table or Page/Table
containing the protected row id is a direct payload witness at that query's
snapshot. Nested typed row returns retain their source-table identity. This
avoids inventing an invalid absent world by deleting a referenced parent.
Projected scalar/aggregate/refusal observations still require validated paired
worlds; a matching untyped id or value alone cannot attribute an exposure.

Check prioritizes actual fixture histories and column observers before searching
remaining synthetic counterexamples. It does not repeat the test-only operation
exploration matrix, which judges no additional policy. That matrix shares an
8192-call budget across every operation (1–512 calls per operation), reports
truncation, and preserves enum/reference/principal alternatives within that cap.
Required image/file operation arguments are actual uploads owned by the acting
principal, with ledger/scratch custody in the isolated engine. Unsupported video
and uncoupled column-world media identities remain named.

For escalation advice, a role-protected appointer is an explicit delegation
boundary; conferring a different role is not by itself evidence of escalation.
Independent role-value intent may restrict that boundary (for example a producer
must not confer legal). Ordinary role casts make service-seat equality false,
including inside operation requirements. Fixed literals still undergo the privilege comparison; ordinary enrollment
whose rules add no role-specific authority is not advised. Analyze caller-chosen
roles, defaulted inserted authority and owner-field upgrades; respect the
argument's actual enum choices. A fixed bootstrap that can appoint further
roles remains an actionable first-admin risk. Notes remain advisory; absence
of independent intent is not proof that a delegation matches the specification.

### 12.8 Runtime intent and independent worlds

Reference-hop intent is judged by the engine's own authorized RuleProbe and
three-valued verdict on an isolated actual snapshot. Hidden/missing references
remain Unknown, never false; snapshot-only evaluators decline those terms.
Temporal reference policies require a per-tick authorized replay and remain
explicitly unsupported until that path runs. Referenced immutable-column worlds
use fresh stores with copied verified asset ledgers and all authored base rows,
then ordinary seed validation/recomputed maintenance. They do not require a
legal delete transition. Every non-protected base field, capture, row identity
and row population must match before comparing observations.

Implicit stability includes source and member writes to row-keyed views,
maintained owner columns, through-parent effects and transitive cascade/set-null.
Only an actual direct delete can refute delete authority; commit-end expiry is
not relabeled a deletion by the caller. Defaulted upsert roles are analyzed on
the insert arm only; omitted fields are preserved on update. Revocability advice
requires an expanded write to a mutable role field or an admitted deletion,
not an unrelated display-name update.

For a write history, a stable necessary policy component may establish a
violation despite another changing conjunct. Replace unstable atoms (or an
entire unstable negation) by Allow, retaining And/Or composition; separately
verify `original => weakened` with the existing exhaustive kernel. Denial of
that weakened condition establishes denial of the original, never its proof.
Stability includes transitive read-authority dependencies of referenced rows.
Do not use this reduction when implicit writes can change the target image or
executed arm. This catches a demoted recorder whose role stays false even when
another statement updates an unrelated field on its parent. No new axiom is
introduced. Id-column presence/count intent shares the row identity observer
path; arbitrary untyped matching ids do not establish a payload witness.

Independent intent binds authorized reference operands without installing a
runtime read gate; live-hop restrictions on executable read rules do not remove
those operands from intent. Probe-key/equality reference hops participate in
stability and transitive authority dependency collection. Every committed
history state rechecks row assertions, including unchanged rows whose related
policy rows changed; pre-existing unchanged seed violations stay supplemental.
Role advice expands helpers with lexical argument substitution and dominating
requirements, retaining the actual write source site. Constant defaults and
## 3. Sound proof boundary

For a table effect, prove `admission => policy` for **every database snapshot**,
row image, ordinary authenticated viewer (including service seats) and clock.
The engine system principal is explicitly outside this quantification: it is
the trusted seed/import/job boundary, never an ordinary user or an implicit
service-seat exemption. Tables touched by system/native/job/worker paths remain
unproved absent a reviewed reduction, and audit lists those writers. This overapproximates every reachable history:
an old assignment and a changed current role are independent facts. Grant
creation never proves a current role. Required role predicates must be true at
the access boundary. No fixture-specific assumption enters a proof.

Translate Boolean connectives compositionally, retaining true/false/unknown
truth for atomic predicates. Unknown never admits and `not unknown` is unknown.
Atomic facts are keyed by their full bound structure, including table, index,
keys, named equalities and old/next image. Symmetric equality may canonicalize by operand reordering only, preserving
Value variants (Id is not Text), table, index, keys, image and predicate kind;
other equalities and probes stay independent. A finite exhaustive implication
check over this abstraction is sound because it includes every concrete
valuation, even impossible ones. Cap atoms/work; cap exhaustion is unproved.
Do not add database consistency axioms without a separate soundness argument.
Never drop, assume true/false, or simplify an unsupported admission atom;
keep it opaque with its full identity or report unproved. Concrete leaf
semantics are the current `evaluate_leaf`/`term`, not stale Lean open points:
null equals null, null differs from a non-null literal, Id differs from Text,
noninteger ordered comparison returns false, and a missing live reference
marks unknown (including under negation). Missing ordinary fields currently
load null; missing reference and missing image are not conflated. Any semantic
mismatch with the independent model is named, never normalized away. Runtime
errors do not establish an admitted result; probe/storage/schema failure
cannot be used as a successful counterexample or a proof fact.

The Lean addition proves a three-valued Kleene kernel with admission only
on Readable and the exact theorem: exhaustive checking implies that for every
valuation, eval(admission) = Readable implies eval(policy) = Readable; the Quarry-to-kernel translation and runtime boundary remain reviewed
Rust obligations, not a claim of whole-platform formal verification.

For read intent, admission includes the ordinary read rule and every bypass:
- point/list/view/filter/count/exists and live refetch use ruled reads;
- sync acquisition and publication authorize at their own server snapshot. An
  offline device cannot know a later server revocation, even with until-revoked.
  A synced read invariant therefore reports the device access path unproved
  for current-server-role intent, separately from its server proof. Delivered
  history has an additional explicit retained-history blocker. No stale local
  role is treated as a current server role;
- inherited read-through rules are checked in their bound form, including
  captures; live-parent semantics remain RULES5's responsibility;
- valued write targets already reread through `get` before returning, and
  generated insert results also use an ordinary ruled read. Verify those
  reductions against the bound IR; any escaping image without that reduction
  is a blocker or must satisfy the implication `effective write admission => read intent` for that image;
  otherwise the declaration stays unproved;
- maintained aggregates/columns and row views may expose source changes under
  different rules: report such source claims unproved unless a reviewed exact
  reduction proves them; projections narrow payload only; existence/count disclosure follows the
  derived path's own rules, so each derived path needs its own reduction or
  stays unproved;
- table-rule probes see raw target rows, unlike program `exists`. Follow
  reverse dependencies into every other table's read/write rules: the presence
  of a marker row or a successful write can disclose the protected target's
  existence or named fields without returning that target. Source privacy
  intent remains unproved for such raw-probe observations unless a reviewed
  reduction proves authorization or the policy explicitly permits disclosure;
- incoming `on delete cascade` and `on delete set null` effects bypass child
  delete/update rules. Report child delete/update/column-write invariants
  unproved when affected, including transitive cascades, until initiating
  authority and child-image policy are modeled. Enumerate these edges in audit;
- asset reads inherit their owning row privacy, while upload/write authority
  belongs to insert/update intent; applicable private-field authorities must be conjoined with owning-row
  admission for asset reads;
- jobs, schedules, native jobs, actions and external effects are trusted system
  or worker boundaries. Read claims affected by opaque outputs/side effects
  remain unproved. Job writes do not prove ordinary-user write authority;
- refusal codes are observations: an authored difference between an absent
  lookup and later denial is a reported exposure. A privacy proof must not
  ignore that path. Unmodeled constraint/refusal observations remain unproved.

Observer boundary: this verifies row payload and logical existence/refusal
observations, not physical timing, runtime cost or all resource side channels.
The platform's documented examined-count/meter-cut observations are explicitly
excluded and reported as exclusions, not full noninterference. Read intent
includes all logical exposures above. A component may be proved while the
whole declaration remains unproved because another path is blocked.

The implementation must build a closed-world census of bound IR read/write
forms and schema-triggered paths. An unclassified form is a blocker, never
silently omitted. Census includes sequence allocation/read admission, online
and device query paths, ephemeral paths, owner fields, maintained writes and
referential actions. System/job/action/native/external touched tables default
unproved without a reviewed reduction, and are listed as grant-column writers.
Every `read` declaration covers all enumerated logical paths, not just its
direct rule. Authenticated viewer classes use engine operations; signed-out
cases run only against HTTP/auth admission, never a fabricated principal.
Retained-history observations are classified by leave policy and reported
separately; they cannot refute the platform's retention contract or prove a
current-role declaration. Test output names unsupported device freshness.

The report enumerates the paths checked and blockers. A proof is never awarded
just because the direct table rule passed while an indirect path is unmodeled.
# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts and five actionable notes; `--verbose` retains every reason.
A mirror invariant cannot catch a wrong rule: the advisory asks
for the spec's audience, resource relationship and lifecycle condition instead.
A legitimate simple owner policy can be equivalent; the advisory never refuses it.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: it offers unconfirmed
per-role/per-column alternatives and questions, never auto-accepted policy.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.

        },_=>return Err("row integrity/transition challenge is not an operation admission test".into()),
    };
    let mut engine=engine(bound,&seeds,probe)?;

    let outcome=engine.mutate(name,None,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    if !matches!(outcome.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..}){return Ok((false,row.clone()));}

    }
